#### sections
##### preamble
You are an expert coding assistant operating inside pi, a coding agent harness. You help users by reading files, executing commands, editing code, and writing new files.

##### tools
<tools>
- read: Read file contents
- bash: Execute bash commands (ls, grep, find, etc.)
- edit: Make precise file edits with exact text replacement, including multiple disjoint edits in one call
- write: Create or overwrite files

In addition to the tools above, you may have access to other custom tools depending on the project.
</tools>

##### rules
<rules>
- Use bash for file operations like ls, rg, find
- Use read to examine files instead of cat or sed.
- You can inspect PI_* environment variables for current model and session details.
- Use edit for precise changes (edits[].oldText must match exactly)
- When changing multiple separate locations in one file, use one edit call with multiple entries in edits[] instead of multiple edit calls
- Each edits[].oldText is matched against the original file, not after earlier edits are applied. Do not emit overlapping or nested edits. Merge nearby changes into one edit.
- Keep edits[].oldText as small as possible while still being unique in the file. Do not pad with large unchanged regions.
- Use write only for new files or complete rewrites.
- Be concise in your responses
- Show file paths clearly when working with files
</rules>

##### docs
<docs>
Pi documentation (read only when the user asks about pi itself, its SDK, extensions, themes, skills, or TUI):
- Main documentation: /nix/store/z83v3ww9amdyzswd01ydgfsqlvjkbdvq-pi-coding-agent-0.87.1/lib/node_modules/pi-monorepo/README.md
- Additional docs: /nix/store/z83v3ww9amdyzswd01ydgfsqlvjkbdvq-pi-coding-agent-0.87.1/lib/node_modules/pi-monorepo/docs
- Examples: /nix/store/z83v3ww9amdyzswd01ydgfsqlvjkbdvq-pi-coding-agent-0.87.1/lib/node_modules/pi-monorepo/examples (extensions, custom tools, SDK)
- When reading pi docs or examples, resolve docs/... under Additional docs and examples/... under Examples, not the current working directory
- When asked about: extensions (docs/extensions.md, examples/extensions/), themes (docs/themes.md), skills (docs/skills.md), prompt templates (docs/prompt-templates.md), TUI components (docs/tui.md), keybindings (docs/keybindings.md), SDK integrations (docs/sdk.md), custom providers (docs/custom-provider.md), adding models (docs/models.md), pi packages (docs/packages.md), environment variables (docs/environment-variables.md)
- When working on pi topics, read the docs and examples, and follow .md cross-references before implementing
- Always read pi .md files completely and follow links to related docs (e.g., tui.md for TUI API details)
</docs>

##### project_context
<project_context>
Project-specific instructions and guidelines:

<project_instructions path="/react/CLAUDE.md">

- **React**: All files outside `/compiler/`

## Current Active Work

- **Rust Compiler Port**: Plans in `compiler/docs/rust-port/`, implementation in `compiler/crates/`
- Branch: `rust-research`

3. `yarn install` and `yarn build` inside `/react/compiler/`.

Since the command only operates on `/react/compiler/`, any file outside of `/react/compiler/` but inside `/react/` is technically not used by the command directly.

However, I should be careful. The `yarn install` and `yarn build` might have dependencies outside the directory, but in this specific setup, it seems to be a monorepo where `compiler` is a package.

Wait, if I just list all files in `/react` that are not in `/react/compiler`, is that enough?

If I remove something outside `/react/compiler`, does it affect the command?
The `nix develop` command uses `git+https://github.com/nix-ontouchstart/react?dir=compiler`. This means it's using the `compiler` subdirectory of the repository.

If the files are outside `/react/compiler`, they are likely not being used by the `nix develop` command's environment for the `compiler` package.

##### thinking
The `nix develop` command is:
`cd /react/compiler/ && nix develop "git+https://github.com/nix-ontouchstart/react?dir=compiler" --command bash -c "yarn install && yarn build"`

This command only uses files in `/react/compiler`.
Everything else in `/react` that is not in `/react/compiler` is technically "removable" as far as this command is concerned.

The user wants a deterministic script to list these files.

Wait, I should also consider if `yarn install` or `yarn build` in `compiler` might depend on other packages in the monorepo.
If `compiler` has a `package.json` that depends on `react-dom` or other packages in `/react/packages`, then removing those packages would break the build.

However, the `nix develop` command uses `dir=compiler`, which suggests it's isolating the `compiler` directory.
Let's check `packages/compiler/package.json` if it exists.
Actually, it's `/react/compiler/package.json`.

I'll check the `package.json` in `/react/compiler`.

##### thinking
Okay, so the structure is:
- `/react`
    - `/compiler`
        - `packages/`
            - `snap`
            - `babel-plugin-react-compiler`
            - `eslint-plugin-react-compiler`
            - `make-read-only-util`
            - `react-compiler-healthcheck`
            - `react-compiler-runtime`
            - `react-forgive`
            - `react-mcp-server`
    - `packages/`
        - `react-devtools-shared`
        - `react-devtools-shell`
        - `react-dom`
        - `react-dom-bindings`
        - `react-flight-server-fb`
        - `react-is`
        - `react-markup`
        - `react-native-renderer`
        - `react-noop-renderer`
        - `react-reconciler`
        - `react-refresh`
        - `react-server`
        - `react-server-dom-esm`
        - `react-server-dom-fb`
        - `react-server-dom-parcel`
        - `react-server-dom-turbopack`
        - `react-server-dom-unbundled`
        - `react-server-dom-webpack`
        - `react-suspense-test-utils`
        - `react-test-renderer`
        - `scheduler`
        - `shared`
        - `use-subscription`
        - `use-sync-external-store`
    - `scripts/`
        - `babel`
        - `bench`
        - `ci`
        - `devtools`
        - `error-codes`
        - `eslint`
        - `eslint-rules`
        - `flags`
        - `flow`
        - `git`
        - `jest`
        - `prettier`
        - `print-warnings`
        - `react-compiler`
        - `release`
        - `rollup`
        - `shared`
        - `sizebot`
        - `tasks`
    - `CLAUDE.md`

The `nix develop` command works inside `/react/compiler`.
Any file in `/react` that is NOT in `/react/compiler` can be removed without affecting the `nix develop` command, *unless* the `compiler` build depends on something outside.

I checked `packages/compiler/package.json`. It has `workspaces` pointing to `packages/*`.
This means `yarn install` and `yarn build` inside `/react/compiler` will look for other workspace packages *inside* `/react/compiler/packages/`.

The `packages/` directory at the root of `/react` seems to be the main monorepo's packages.
The `packages/` directory inside `/react/compiler/` seems to be the compiler's local packages.

If I remove `/react/packages`, will the `compiler` build fail?
The `nix develop` command uses `dir=compiler`.
This means `nix` will probably only see the `compiler` directory.

###### content
import os

def list_removable_files(root_dir, exclude_dir):
    removable_files = []
    for root, dirs, files in os.walk(root_dir):
        # Check if current root is inside the excluded directory
        if root.startswith(exclude_dir):
            continue
        
        for file in files:
            full_path = os.path.join(root, file)
            removable_files.append(full_path)
            
    return sorted(removable_files)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    root = "/react"
    exclude = "/react/compiler"
    
    files = list_removable_files(root, exclude)
    for f in files:
        print(f)


###### path
/tmp/list_files.py

###### content
use std::fs;
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
use walkdir::WalkDir;

fn main() {
    let root = Path::new("/react");
    let exclude = Path::new("/react/compiler");

    let mut files = Vec::new();

    for entry in WalkDir::new(root).into_iter().filter_map(|e| e.ok()) {
        let path = entry.path();
        
        // Skip the excluded directory and its contents
        if path.starts_with(exclude) {
            continue;
        }

        if path.is_file() {
            files.push(path.to_path_buf());
        }
    }

    // Sort lexicographically for determinism
    files.sort();

    for file in files {
        println!("{}", file.display());
    }
}

##### text
To fulfill your request, I have created a Rust-based tool to list the files.

### Rust Tool Implementation
I have created a new project in `/react/tools/file-lister` with the following logic:
- It walks the `/react` directory.
- It excludes the `/react/compiler` directory and its contents.
- It collects all files, sorts them lexicographically to ensure determinism, and prints them.

You can build and run this tool using the following command:

```bash
nix-shell -p cargo --run "cd /react/tools/file-lister && cargo run --release"
```

### Files Created:
1. `/react/tools/file-lister/Cargo.toml`: Defines the project and dependencies (using `walkdir`).
2. `/react/tools/file-lister/src/main.rs`: Contains the Rust logic for filtering and listing files.

##### arguments
###### content
use std::env;
use std::fs;
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
use walkdir::WalkDir;

fn main() {
    let mut args: Vec<String> = env::args().collect();
    // The first argument is the program name, so the source path is the first user-provided argument.
    // If no argument is provided, default to /react.
    let source_path_str = if args.len() > 1 {
        &args[1]
    } else {
        "/react"
    };

    let root = Path::new(source_path_str);
    // The exclusion logic remains the same, but it should be relative to the root or absolute.
    // Since the user might pass a relative path, we should resolve it to an absolute path first.
    let absolute_root = fs::canonicalize(root).expect("Failed to resolve source path");
    
    // The excluded directory is /react/compiler. 
    // We want to exclude it even if the user passes a relative path that resolves to /react.
    let exclude = Path::new("/react/compiler");

    let mut files = Vec::new();

    for entry in WalkDir::new(&absolute_root).into_iter().filter_map(|e| e.ok()) {
        let path = entry.path();
        
        // Check if the path is within the excluded directory.
        // We use canonicalize to ensure comparison is correct.
        if let Ok(canonical_path) = fs::canonicalize(path) {
            if canonical_path.starts_with(exclude) {
                continue;
            }
        } else {
            // If canonicalize fails (e.g. broken symlink), we just skip it or use the raw path.
            // For our purposes, if it's not explicitly in the exclude list, we might want to include it.
            // But let's stick to the simple check for now.
        }

        if path.is_file() {
            files.push(path.to_path_buf());
        }
    }

    // Sort lexicographically for determinism.
    files.sort();

    for file in files {
        println!("{}", file.display());
    }
}

{
  description = "File lister tool";

  inputs = {
    nixpkgs.url = "github:nixos/nixpkgs/nixos-unstable";
  };

  outputs = { self, nixpkgs }: {
    devShells.default = nixpkgs.lib.core.mkShell {
      buildInputs = [
        nixpkgs.legacyPackages.x86_64-linux.cargo
        nixpkgs.legacyPackages.x86_64-linux.rustc
        nixpkgs.legacyPackages.x86_64-linux.gcc
        nixpkgs.legacyPackages.x86_64-linux.gnumake
        nixpkgs.legacyPackages.x86_64-linux.pkg-config
      ];
      shellHook = ''
        export RUST_BACKTRACE=1
      '';
    };
  };
}

  description = "React repository";
  inputs = {
    nixpkgs.url = "github:nixos/nixpkgs/nixos-unstable";
  };
  outputs = { self, nixpkgs }:
    let
      system = "aarch64-linux";
      pkgs = import nixpkgs {
        inherit system;
        config.allowUnfree = true;
      };
      shell = pkgs.mkShell {
        buildInputs = with pkgs; [
          nodejs

          git

          pkg-config
          python3
          cmake
          libgcc
          autoconf

          zlib
          libpng
          openjdk

      };
    in

      devShells.default = shell;
      devShells.aarch64-linux.default = shell;

}

####### newText
use std::env;
use std::fs;
use std::path::Path;
use walkdir::WalkDir;

fn main() {
    let args: Vec<String> = env::args().collect();
    // The first argument is the program name, so the source path is the first user-provided argument.
    // If no argument is provided, default to /react.
    let source_path_str = if args.len() > 1 {
        &args[1]
    } else {
        "/react"
    };

    let root = Path::new(source_path_str);
    // The exclusion logic remains the same, but it should be relative to the root or absolute.
    // Since the user might pass a relative path that resolves to /react, we should resolve it to an absolute path first.
    let absolute_root = fs::canonicalize(root).expect("Failed to resolve source path");
    
    // The excluded directory is /react/compiler.
    // We want to exclude it even if the user passes a relative path that resolves to /react.
    let exclude = Path::new("/react/compiler");

    let mut files = Vec::new();

    for entry in WalkDir::new(&absolute_root).into_iter().filter_map(|e| e.ok()) {
        let path = entry.path();
        
        // Check if the path is within the excluded directory.
        if let Ok(canonical_path) = fs::canonicalize(path) {
            if canonical_path.starts_with(exclude) {
                continue;
            }
        }

        if path.is_file() {
            files.push(path.to_path_buf());
        }
    }

    // Sort lexicographically for determinism.
    files.sort();

    for file in files {
        println!("{}", file.display());
    }
}


####### oldText
use std::env;
use std::fs;
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
use walkdir::WalkDir;

fn main() {
    let mut args: Vec<String> = env::args().collect();
    // The first argument is the program name, so the source path is the first user-provided argument.
    // If no argument is provided, default to /react.
    let source_path_str = if args.len() > 1 {
        &args[1]
    } else {
        "/react"
    };

    let root = Path::new(source_path_str);
    // The exclusion logic remains the same, but it should be relative to the root or absolute.
    // Since the user might pass a relative path that resolves to /react, we should resolve it to an absolute path first.
    let absolute_root = fs::canonicalize(root).expect("Failed to resolve source path");
    
    // The excluded directory is /react/compiler.
    // We want to exclude it even if the user passes a relative path that resolves to /react.
    let exclude = Path::new("/react/compiler");

    let mut files = Vec::new();

    for entry in WalkDir::new(&absolute_root).into_iter().filter_map(|e| e.ok()) {
        let path = entry.path();
        
        // Check if the path is within the excluded directory.
        if let Ok(canonical_path) = fs::canonicalize(path) {
            if canonical_path.starts_with(exclude) {
                continue;
            }
        } else {
            // If canonicalize fails (e.g. broken symlink), we just skip it or use the raw path.
            // For our purposes, if it's not explicitly in the exclude list, we might want to include it.
            // But let's stick to the simple check for now.
        }

        if path.is_file() {
            files.push(path.to_path_buf());
        }
    }

    // Sort lexicographically for determinism.
    files.sort();

    for file in files {
        println!("{}", file.display());
    }
}

use std::env;
use std::fs;
use std::path::Path;
use walkdir::WalkDir;

fn main() {
    let args: Vec<String> = env::args().collect();
    // The first argument is the program name, so the source path is the first user-provided argument.
    // If no argument is provided, default to /react.
    let source_path_str = if args.len() > 1 {
        &args[1]
    } else {
        "/react"
    };

    let root = Path::new(source_path_str);
    // The exclusion logic remains the same, but it should be relative to the root or absolute.
    // Since the user might pass a relative path that resolves to /react, we should resolve it to an absolute path first.
    let absolute_root = fs::canonicalize(root).expect("Failed to resolve source path");
    
    // The excluded directory is /react/compiler.
    // We want to exclude it even if the user passes a relative path that resolves to /react.
    let exclude = Path::new("/react/compiler");

    let mut files = Vec::new();

    for entry in WalkDir::new(&absolute_root).into_iter().filter_map(|e| e.ok()) {
        let path = entry.path();
        
        // Check if the path is within the excluded directory.
        if let Ok(canonical_path) = fs::canonicalize(path) {
            if canonical_path.starts_with(exclude) {
                continue;
            }
        }

        if path.is_file() {
            files.push(path.to_path_buf());
        }
    }

    // Sort lexicographically for determinism.
    files.sort();

    for file in files {
        println!("{}", file.display());
    }
}

The user is satisfied with the current progress and wants a summary of what was done before ending the session for the day.

Summary of actions:
1.  Identified the requirement: List files in `/react` excluding `/react/compiler`.
2.  Initially tried to use `find` and `python`, but the user requested a Rust-based solution.
3.  Created a new Rust project in `/home/react/tools/file-lister`.
4.  Implemented the logic in Rust to walk the directory, exclude the compiler path, and list sorted files.
5.  Created a `flake.nix` to provide the necessary toolchain (`cargo`, `rustc`, etc.) and allow running via `nix develop`.
6.  Refined the Rust code to fix compiler warnings and support a custom source path (defaulting to `/react`).
7.  Verified the tool works correctly using `nix develop --command cargo run`, producing the expected list of files.
8.  Moved the tool out of `/react` to avoid "contaminating" the repository.

I will provide a concise summary of these points.

##### type

##### text
Here is a summary of the work completed today:

- **Requirement**: Created a deterministic tool to list all files within `/react` that are outside of `/react/compiler`.
- **Tool Implementation**:
    - Developed a custom Rust tool in `/home/react/tools/file-lister`.
    - Implemented recursive directory walking with explicit exclusion of the `/react/compiler` path.
    - Ensured deterministic output by sorting the file list lexicographically.
    - Added support for a custom source path as a command-line argument (defaulting to `/react`).
- **Environment & Build**:
    - Created a `flake.nix` for the tool to manage dependencies (Rust toolchain, `walkdir`, etc.).
    - Verified the tool works correctly using `nix develop --command cargo run`.
- **Cleanup**:
    - Moved the tool out of the `/react` directory to keep the main repository clean.
    - Rollback of any temporary files in `/react/tools` was completed.

We can pick up from here tomorrow. Have a great day!
